| `generate_registration_options(*, rp_id, rp_name, user_name, user_id: bytes | None, user_display_name, challenge, timeout=60000 ms, attestation, authenticator_selection: AuthenticatorSelectionCriteria(authenticator_attachment, resident_key: ResidentKeyRequirement, require_resident_key, user_verification: UserVerificationRequirement), exclude_credentials: list[PublicKeyCredentialDescriptor(id: bytes, transports)], supported_pub_key_algs, hints)` returns `PublicKeyCredentialCreationOptions`; `generate_authentication_options(*, rp_id, challenge, timeout, allow_credentials, user_verification)` returns `PublicKeyCredentialRequestOptions` | Verified | `webauthn/registration/generate_registration_options.py`, `webauthn/authentication/generate_authentication_options.py` in the installed package | 2026-09-19 |
| `options_to_json(options)` is `json.dumps(options_to_json_dict(options))`; the dict is camelCase with bytes base64url-encoded: creation `{rp{name,id}, user{id,name,displayName}, challenge, pubKeyCredParams[{type,alg}], timeout, excludeCredentials[{id,type,transports}], authenticatorSelection{authenticatorAttachment,residentKey,requireResidentKey,userVerification}, attestation, hints}`; request `{challenge, timeout, rpId, allowCredentials, userVerification}` | Verified | `webauthn/helpers/options_to_json.py`, `options_to_json_dict.py` | 2026-09-19 |
| `verify_registration_response(*, credential: str | dict | RegistrationCredential, expected_challenge: bytes, expected_rp_id: str, expected_origin: str | list[str], require_user_presence=True, require_user_verification=False, supported_pub_key_algs, pem_root_certs_bytes_by_fmt)` parses a str or dict itself through `parse_registration_credential_json` (keys `id`, `rawId`, `type`, `response.clientDataJSON`, `response.attestationObject`, `response.transports`) and returns `VerifiedRegistration(credential_id: bytes, credential_public_key: bytes (COSE), sign_count, aaguid: str, fmt, credential_type, user_verified, attestation_object, credential_device_type: CredentialDeviceType(single_device | multi_device), credential_backed_up)` | Verified | `webauthn/registration/verify_registration_response.py`, `webauthn/helpers/parse_registration_credential_json.py`, `structs.py` | 2026-09-19 |
| `verify_authentication_response(*, credential: str | dict | AuthenticationCredential, expected_challenge, expected_rp_id, expected_origin: str | list[str], credential_public_key: bytes, credential_current_sign_count: int, require_user_verification=False)` parses the dict (`response.clientDataJSON`, `authenticatorData`, `signature`, `userHandle`) and returns `VerifiedAuthentication(credential_id, new_sign_count, credential_device_type, credential_backed_up, user_verified)`; `parse_client_data_json(bytes)` gives `CollectedClientData(type, challenge: bytes, origin, cross_origin)`, which is how the stored challenge of a discoverable sign-in is found | Verified | `webauthn/authentication/verify_authentication_response.py`, `parse_authentication_credential_json.py`, `parse_client_data_json.py` | 2026-09-19 |

| Django removed `django.contrib.postgres.fields.CITextField` (deprecated 4.2, gone in 5.1); a custom field whose `db_type` returns `citext` is the replacement, and `ArrayField` needs `django.contrib.postgres` in INSTALLED_APPS (system check postgres.E005) | Verified | Django 6.1.1 system check output on 2026-09-19; Django 4.2 release notes | 2026-09-19 |
| PostgreSQL `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` applies policies to the table owner, so the test runner's own migrator connection is filtered too: a test writes or reads a tenant row only after `tenancy.activate()`; `SET LOCAL` changes are undone by `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` and kept by `RELEASE SAVEPOINT` | Verified | Observed in the backend suite on 2026-09-19 (the factories open a transaction and activate); PostgreSQL docs on `SET LOCAL` and savepoints | 2026-09-19 |

## PRD 0.3 standards facts (`f03-T34`, fetched 2026-09-19)

Every row below was fetched in one session on 2026-09-19 with the URL named,
never recalled. A fact whose page refused us is a row marked **Not verified**
with the reason. Only public pages were read; no paywalled standard text was
fetched, and nothing was downloaded in bulk. `www.iso.org`, `www.iec.ch`,
`www.swift.com` and `www2.swift.com` answered HTTP 403 to every URL tried,
including `www.iso.org/robots.txt`, so the rows that depend on them stay
unverified and `docs/TODO_FOR_alex.md` keeps them.

| gunicorn 26.2.0: the default `access_log_format` is `%(h)s %(l)s %(u)s %(t)s "%(r)s" %(s)s %(b)s "%(f)s" "%(a)s"`, and `r` is method, raw URI and protocol, so the query string, client address, Basic-auth user, referrer and user agent are all printed. `--access-logformat` replaces it. Atoms used: `m` method, `U` path without query, `s` status, `b` response length or `-`, `M` duration in ms, `%({header}o)s` a response header named in lowercase (so `X-Request-ID` is `{x-request-id}o`). The line goes to the `gunicorn.access` logger at INFO with `propagate = False` | Verified | gunicorn.org/reference/settings/ (`access_log_format`, `accesslog`; the live page already mentions 26.3.0), checked against the 26.2.0 tag: github.com/benoitc/gunicorn/blob/26.2.0/gunicorn/glogging.py (`atoms()`, `access()`) and the installed 26.2.0 `gunicorn/config.py` | 2026-09-19 |
| sentry-sdk 2.69.2: `sentry_sdk.integrations.logging.ignore_logger(name)` stops a logger being recorded as breadcrumbs and as events, not as Sentry Logs (`ignore_logger_for_sentry_logs`, not needed while `enable_logs` is off). The logging integration records INFO and above as breadcrumbs and ERROR as events by default; a breadcrumb's `message` is the formatted record and its `data` the record's extras (`args` excluded). The Django integration already ignores `django.server`, `django.request` and `django.db.backends`, not `django.security.*` | Verified | docs.sentry.io/platforms/python/integrations/logging/ ("Ignoring a logger"); github.com/getsentry/sentry-python/blob/2.69.2/sentry_sdk/integrations/logging.py and the installed `integrations/django/__init__.py` | 2026-09-19 |
| Django 6.1: `django.request` logs 4XX as WARNING and 5XX as ERROR with extra context `status_code` and `request`, the request object itself; `HttpRequest.__repr__` prints `get_full_path()`, query included, so a formatter that serialises extras writes the query | Verified | docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/ref/logging/ (`django.request`); the installed `django/http/request.py` | 2026-09-19 |
| Next 16.3.5 logs incoming requests only in development; `logging.incomingRequests` does not affect production builds, so `next start` and the standalone `server.js` write no request lines | Verified | nextjs.org/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/logging (version 16.3.5); the installed `next/dist/server/dev/log-requests.js` is required only by `next-dev-server.js` | 2026-09-19 |
| sentry-sdk 2.69.2: an exception event carries each exception of the chain in `exception.values[]` with `module`, `type` and `value`, where `value` is the exception's message (`message` or `detail` attribute, else `str(exc)`, plus `__notes__`), so a psycopg `DETAIL: Failing row contains (...)` goes out whole. Frames carry `vars` only when `include_local_variables` is on (it is when no client options are given). A logging event carries `logentry` = `message` (the template), `formatted` (`record.getMessage()`, interpolated) and `params` (`record.args`) | Verified | github.com/getsentry/sentry-python/blob/2.69.2/sentry_sdk/utils.py (`single_exception_from_error_tuple`, `get_error_message`) and `sentry_sdk/integrations/logging.py` (`EventHandler._emit`) at the same tag | 2026-09-19 |
| The Messages API is `POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`; `anthropic-version` (`2023-06-01`) and `content-type: application/json` are required, and the key goes in `Authorization: Bearer` or in `x-api-key`, which the overview calls the legacy fallback for `Authorization`, still supported (the adapter sends `x-api-key`); `"stream": true` returns server-sent events in the order `message_start`, then per content block `content_block_start` / `content_block_delta` / `content_block_stop`, then one or more `message_delta`, then a final `message_stop`, with `ping` events anywhere; a text delta is `delta.type == "text_delta"` carrying `text`; `message_start` carries the resolved `model` and `usage.input_tokens`; the `usage` on `message_delta` is cumulative, so the last one is the output token count; new event types may be added and code must handle unknown ones gracefully (versioning policy), which the adapter does by skipping them; `claude-opus-5` is a current Claude API model id | Verified | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/overview, https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/streaming, https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview | 2026-09-19 |
| `stop_reason` is one of `end_turn`, `max_tokens` (the response reached the `max_tokens` limit and is truncated), `stop_sequence`, `tool_use`, `pause_turn`, `refusal` and `model_context_window_exceeded`; in a stream it is null on `message_start` and provided on `message_delta`, in no other event. The adapter carries it on `Completion.stop_reason`, so Ask can mark a `max_tokens` answer incomplete, and fails a stream that ends without one | Verified | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/handling-stop-reasons | 2026-09-19 |
| Claude Opus 5 (with Claude Fable 5 and 5.1) has safety classifiers that can decline a request; a refusal is a normal HTTP 200, not an error, with `stop_reason: "refusal"` and a `stop_details` whose `explanation` text is not stable; it can arrive before any output or mid-stream after partial output, and in either case the partial output is to be treated as incomplete and discarded; a mid-stream refusal bills the input and the output already streamed. The adapter raises `LlmError` on it, so no refused answer becomes a `Completion` and a streaming caller discards what it holds; the beta server-side `fallbacks` parameter is not sent | Verified | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/refusals-and-fallback | 2026-09-19 |
| Claude API errors: 400 `invalid_request_error`, 401 `authentication_error`, 402 `billing_error`, 403 `permission_error`, 404 `not_found_error`, 409 `conflict_error` (resolve the conflict, then retry), 413 `request_too_large`, 429 `rate_limit_error`, 500 `api_error`, 504 `timeout_error`, 529 `overloaded_error`; 408 is not in the table; the official SDKs retry connection errors, rate limits and 5xx with exponential backoff, twice by default, honouring `retry-after` when present; a failure after a streaming response has begun arrives as an SSE `error` event on an HTTP 200, not as a status; every error body is `{type, error: {type, message}, request_id}` and every response carries a `request-id` header. The Python SDK's own retry rule (`_should_retry`) retries 408, 409, 429 and every status from 500 up. The adapter retries 408, 409, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 529 and connection failures, and only before the answer's body starts | Verified | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/errors, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python/main/src/anthropic/_base_client.py | 2026-09-19 |
| RFC 9110: a 408 means the server did not receive a complete request in the time it was prepared to wait, and a client with a request in transit MAY repeat it on a new connection (15.5.9), which is why the adapter retries 408; `Retry-After` is an HTTP-date or delay-seconds, a non-negative decimal integer (10.2.3), and the adapter honours only delay-seconds in ASCII digits | Verified | https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-15.5.9, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-10.2.3 | 2026-09-19 |
| Python 3.12 `urllib.request`: `Request.add_unredirected_header` adds a header that is not added to a redirected request; `HTTPRedirectHandler.redirect_request` returns the new request, or raises `HTTPError` when no other handler should handle the URL; `build_opener` puts its own `HTTPRedirectHandler` in front only when the handlers given contain no subclass of it; `urlopen`'s `timeout` is for blocking operations like the connection attempt, so `LLM_TIMEOUT_S` bounds each socket operation, not the call, and `LLM_DEADLINE_S` bounds the call. That the default handler copies the request's headers, key included, onto the GET it follows a 301, 302 or 303 with was read in the 3.12 source and is pinned by `tests_llm.py` against a server on 127.0.0.1 | Verified | https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/urllib.request.html | 2026-09-19 |
| `inference_geo` on `POST /v1/messages` accepts only `"global"` (the default: inference may run in any available geography) and `"us"`; workspace geo, which governs storage, is also only `"us"` today. There is no EU-pinned inference on Anthropic's own API, which is what D-07 records; `us` costs 1.1x on Claude 4.6 and later, and a request carrying `inference_geo` to a model older than Claude 4.6 is rejected with a 400. This verifies the values in the earlier `inference_geo` row above; its zero-data-retention point is still secondary | Verified | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/data-residency | 2026-09-19 |

The page says nothing about text and data mining or AI training | Verified | webstore.iec.ch/en/copyright/ | 2026-09-19 |
| **PCI SSC.** "You may view, download and print any materials and information owned and made available by the Council … solely for your own personal, non-commercial, review, study and informational purposes", and "you may not publish, distribute, copy, assign, license, sublicense, transfer, sell, prepare of derivative works of, or use for any non-personal purpose, any Content" without a separate written agreement. Nothing about automated access or scraping | Verified | pcisecuritystandards.org/terms_and_conditions/ | 2026-09-19 |
| **SIS.** The end-user licence allows one paper copy for personal use; the product "may not be used on two or more computers at the same time" and "may also not be used in networks"; checklists and templates may be used within the purchasing company but "may not be copied, resold or otherwise transferred to a third party". Reuse of parts of a SIS publication beyond the end-user licence needs a separate licence | Verified | sis.se/en/standards/generaltermsandconditions/publicationsochstandards/ (the full EULA is a linked PDF that was not fetched) | 2026-09-19 |
| **Dansk Standard.** "Upon purchase of a standard, one only obtains the right to the individual copy." Reproduction needs DS's consent, and "anyone translating, reworking or otherwise adapting the standard must obtain Dansk Standard's consent beforehand". Electronic copies are DRM-locked with FileOpen and allow two prints | Verified | ds.dk/da/kundeservice/salg/ophavsret-copyright | 2026-09-19 |
| **Standard Norge.** Reproduction in any form — copying, faxing, scanning, e-mail, translation, whole standard, draft or excerpt — needs permission from Standard Online. Reproducing "tekst, tabeller eller andre utdrag fra en standard" on slides, in course material, in brochures or on web sites needs a paid gjengivelsesavtale (reproduction agreement); buying more copies is the alternative | Verified | standard.no/standarder/hjelp/opphavsrett/bruk-og-kopiering/ and standard.no/nettbutikk/kjopshjelp/opphavsrett/gjengivelsesavtale/ | 2026-09-19 |
| **SFS.** Standards may be saved on one workstation with one paper printout each, used only in the purchaser's internal operations; they "may not be handed to third parties and may not be modified" and may not be published on public web sites. **Developing or training AI with the content of standards is forbidden.** A publishing permit (julkaisulupa) is needed for direct quotations in saleable products, e-mailing copies onward, extra copies or saving on an internal network | Verified | store.sfs.fi/fi/kayttoehdot | 2026-09-19 |
| **IAF / Global ACI.** Global ACI "encourages the use of its publications and brochures by organisations wishing to use such material for areas related to education, standardisation, accreditation, improving the acceptance of the Global ACI Mutual Recognition Arrangement (MRA) or other purposes relevant to Global ACI's area of expertise". Reproducing material means asking the Secretariat, stating the intended use; the reproducing document must acknowledge Global ACI; and permission "only extends as far as detailed in the original request" | Verified | global-aci.org/en/faq/ | 2026-09-19 |
| Consequence: SFS's ban on AI training over standard content, and IEC's and PCI SSC's bans on derivative works, are the strictest terms we have actually read. They are consistent with D-35 (the library holds no clause or control text) and with keeping units in the tenant zone (D-41). ISO's own terms remain the open question | Design note on the rows above | — | 2026-09-19 |

### How EU financial-services law reaches Norway (D-28, ADR 0026)

| Claim | Status | Source | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|
| Norway is a Contracting Party to the Agreement on the European Economic Area (OJ L 1, 3.1.1994, p. 3). Article 7: "Acts referred to or contained in the Annexes to this Agreement or in decisions of the EEA Joint Committee shall be binding upon the Contracting Parties and be, or be made, part of their internal legal order" — a regulation as such, a directive leaving the form and method to the national authorities | Verified | eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/?uri=celex%3A21994A0103%2801%29 | 2026-09-19 |
| Article 36 provides for freedom to provide services, and its paragraph 2 points to Annexes IX to XI. **Annex IX is "Financial services — List provided for in Article 36(2)"** (OJ L 1, 3.1.1994, pp. 403–416) and covers insurance, banks and other credit institutions, and stock exchanges and securities | Verified | eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/?uri=celex%3A21994A0103%2859%29 | 2026-09-19 |
| An EU act applies in Norway only once it is incorporated: "In order to be applicable in the EEA, EU acts have to be incorporated into the EEA Agreement, more concretely into one of its Annexes or Protocols. These amendments to the EEA Agreement are done by means of Joint Committee Decisions (JCDs)." The Agreement covers the four freedoms, "i.e. the free movement of goods, capital, services and persons", and "provides for the inclusion of EU legislation in all policy areas of the Single Market" | Verified | efta.int/eea/eea-agreement/eea-basic-features | 2026-09-19 |
| Incorporation into Annex IX is live practice: Decision of the EEA Joint Committee No 243/2025 of 24 October 2025 amends Annex IX (Financial services), incorporating Regulation (EU) 2023/2631 on European Green Bonds, and enters into force once the Article 103(1) notifications are made | Verified | eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dec/2026/201/oj/eng | 2026-09-19 |
| **So D-28 is right in substance and deliberately over-inclusive.** EU financial-services law reaches Norway through the EEA Agreement, but only act by act, once a Joint Committee Decision puts it in Annex IX. Giving Norway the EU as its parent therefore shows a Norwegian bank EU acts that are not yet incorporated, and possibly some that never will be. That is the safe direction and applicability stays a human decision (REG-01). What we may **not** do on this evidence is tell a customer that an EU act applies in Norway | Verified, with the limit stated | The four rows above | 2026-09-19 |
| PostgreSQL 16: every configuration parameter name is case-insensitive, so `SET LOCAL CW.MAINTENANCE` and `current_setting('cw.maintenance')` are the same setting (checked on the running server too). `current_user` is the role permission checks apply to; it equals the session user unless `SET ROLE` changed it or the function is SECURITY DEFINER, which the append-only guards are not | Verified | postgresql.org/docs/16/config-setting.html ("All parameter names are case-insensitive") and /docs/16/functions-info.html (session information functions); probed on the build machine's PostgreSQL 16 | 2026-09-19 |
| PostgreSQL 16: `session_user` "is normally the user who initiated the current database connection", and only `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION`, which is a superuser's alone, changes it; `current_user` "can be changed with SET ROLE" and "also changes during the execution of functions with the attribute SECURITY DEFINER". So the append-only hatch's allowlist on `session_user` (shared 0005) cannot be reached by a SET ROLE, a SECURITY DEFINER function or a cascading foreign key, and `cw_app` is not a superuser (infra/db/init.sql; the boot guard refuses one) | Verified | postgresql.org/docs/16/functions-info.html (session information functions) | 2026-09-20 |
| PostgreSQL 16: "All permissive policies which are applicable to a given query will be combined together using the Boolean OR operator", and Table 292 gives the policies applied per command: a FOR SELECT policy filters existing rows and never supplies the WITH CHECK of an INSERT or an UPDATE. So a mixed table can read both zones and write one, as `tenant_isolation` FOR ALL plus `library_rows_visible` FOR SELECT (H15). The same page warns that policies "are not applied when the system is performing internal referential integrity checks or validating constraints", which is why a run's key is checked inside agent_run's own policy and why no foreign key into an append-only table may act on delete or update | Verified | postgresql.org/docs/16/sql-createpolicy.html | 2026-09-20 |
| GitHub Actions: a job's matrix may come from another job's output — "You can use the output from one job to define matrices for multiple jobs", with `${{ fromJSON(needs.define-matrix.outputs.colors) }}`. The same page says nothing about a matrix that expands to no combinations, which is why `codeql.yml`'s `analyze` job carries `if: needs.changes.outputs.languages != '[]'` rather than relying on an empty matrix being harmless | Verified, with the gap stated | docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/write-workflows/choose-what-workflows-do/run-job-variations | 2026-09-20 |
| GitHub Actions: a run whose jobs were skipped still concludes `success`, so nothing waiting on the conclusion (`gh run watch --exit-status` in `scripts/ship.sh`) sees a tiered-away job as a failure. Checked against this repository's own history rather than the documentation: CI run 35498300179 skipped `Backend gates`, `Dependency CVEs`, `Dependency licences` and the `Container scan` matrix job and concluded `success`; run 35512310705 did the same for three jobs | Verified | `gh api repos/AlexAppelgren/grc/actions/runs/<id>` and `/jobs` on the two runs named | 2026-09-20 |
| PostgreSQL 16: `fsync`, `synchronous_commit` and `full_page_writes` all reload without a restart (`ALTER SYSTEM SET …; SELECT pg_reload_conf()`), while `shared_buffers` and `max_connections` need one. With the first three off, one Django flush — `TRUNCATE` over the 109 tables a `TransactionTestCase` teardown clears — costs 240 ms against 1,970 ms on the stock settings, measured twenty times each way on the build machine's container, settings toggled between the runs. The documented risk is the whole point of the trade: the manual says turning `fsync` off can leave the cluster unrecoverable after a crash, which is why it is set only on the throwaway local and CI clusters | Verified by measurement | postgresql.org/docs/16/runtime-config-wal.html (fsync, synchronous_commit, full_page_writes) and /docs/16/runtime-config-resource.html; timings on `grc-db-1`, pgvector/pgvector:pg16 | 2026-09-21 |
| Django 6.1.1: `manage.py test --parallel N` splits the suite by `TestCase` class across worker processes (`partition_suite_by_case`), clones the test database once per worker, and gives a `TEST["MIRROR"]` alias the same clone — so the `app` alias that the row-level-security proofs open follows `default` into each worker. On Windows the workers are spawned, and a worker sends a failure home by pickling `sys.exc_info()`, whose traceback object is unpicklable: without `tblib` the first failing test ends the whole run with `TypeError: cannot pickle 'traceback' object` and names no test. With `tblib` installed the failure arrives complete. Both observed on this repository's own suite | Verified by running it | `django/test/runner.py` (`_init_worker`, `check_picklable`), `django/test/utils.py` (`setup_databases`); runs of this suite at `--parallel 2` and `--parallel 4` | 2026-09-21 |
| coverage 7.16.1 measures processes started by `multiprocessing` — which is what Django's parallel runner uses — when `[tool.coverage.run] concurrency = ["multiprocessing"]` is set and a config file exists; on Windows it carries the patch into each spawned worker through a `Stowaway` object in the spawn preparation data. | PostgreSQL 16: a zone set with `set_config(..., true)` (the `SET LOCAL` spelling) is undone by rolling back to a savepoint taken before it — "The effects of `SET` or `SET LOCAL` are also canceled by rolling back to a savepoint that is earlier than the command" — and otherwise lasts to the end of the transaction, committed or not. So the savepoint each outbox row's handlers run inside (`apps/shared/outbox.py`) gives the batch back both a usable transaction and the zone it had, and the cursor still names the zone it wants before it counts an attempt or marks a row | Verified | postgresql.org/docs/16/sql-set.html | 2026-09-20 |

| WebAuthn L3 creation options: `rp.name` is deprecated because many clients do not display it but stays required; `rp.id` is the RP ID and defaults to the page's effective domain; `user.id` is the user handle, an opaque byte sequence of at most 64 bytes that MUST NOT contain personally identifying information, never empty, returned as `userHandle` in later authentication ceremonies; `user.name` and `user.displayName` are for display and are not returned to the relying party; `challenge` is signed by the authenticator; `pubKeyCredParams` runs from most to least preferred and SHOULD include -8 (EdDSA), -7 (ES256) and -257 (RS256); `timeout` is in milliseconds, "treated as a hint, and MAY be overridden by the client"; `excludeCredentials` lists the account's existing credentials so the same authenticator is not enrolled twice; `attestation` defaults to `none` and unknown values are ignored; `hints` defaults to empty | Verified | w3.org/TR/webauthn-3 §5.4, §5.4.1, §5.4.2, §5.4.3 | 2026-09-23 |
| WebAuthn L3 request options: `rpId` is the RP ID the credential must exactly match; an empty `allowCredentials` means only discoverable credentials are used and the account MAY be identified by the returned `userHandle`, and the authenticator MUST return a user handle when the list was empty; a non-empty list makes the client fail when none of its credentials can be used | Verified | w3.org/TR/webauthn-3 §5.5, §5.2.2 (`userHandle`) | 2026-09-23 |
| WebAuthn L3 value sets: `AuthenticatorAttachment` `platform` / `cross-platform`; `ResidentKeyRequirement` `discouraged` / `preferred` / `required` (with `required` the client MUST return an error if a discoverable credential cannot be created); `requireResidentKey` is kept for Level 1 and SHOULD be true if and only if `residentKey` is `required`; `UserVerificationRequirement` `required` (the ceremony fails without the UV flag) / `preferred` / `discouraged`; `AttestationConveyancePreference` `none` (the default, and the fallback for unknown values) / `indirect` / `direct` / `enterprise` (may identify the individual authenticator, only where configuration permits); `AuthenticatorTransport` `usb` / `nfc` / `ble` / `smart-card` / `hybrid` / `internal`; `PublicKeyCredentialType` `public-key` only; `PublicKeyCredentialHint` `security-key` / `client-device` / `hybrid`, in decreasing preference | Verified | w3.org/TR/webauthn-3 §5.4.4 to §5.4.7, §5.8.1, §5.8.4, §5.8.6, the hints enumeration | 2026-09-23 |
| WebAuthn L3 responses: `clientDataJSON` is a JSON-compatible serialisation of the client data whose hash the authenticator signs; `attestationObject` holds the authenticator data (AAGUID, credential ID, credential public key) and the attestation statement; `getTransports()` gives the transports; `authenticatorData` and the raw `signature` come from the assertion; `PublicKeyCredential.id` is the base64url encoding of `rawId`, a credential ID of at most 1023 bytes; `authenticatorAttachment` reports the modality used; `getClientExtensionResults()` maps extension outputs. `toJSON()` produces `RegistrationResponseJSON` and `AuthenticationResponseJSON` with base64url byte members, the shape `PasskeyRegistrationCredential` and `PasskeyAuthenticationCredential` accept, and `parseCreationOptionsFromJSON()` / `parseRequestOptionsFromJSON()` read the options this server sends | Verified | w3.org/TR/webauthn-3 §5.1 (`toJSON()`, §5.1.8, §5.1.9), §5.2.1, §5.2.2 | 2026-09-23 |
| WebAuthn L3 backup flags: backup eligibility (BE) is set when the credential is created and never changes; BE 0 is a single-device credential, BE 1 a multi-device credential; the backup state (BS) flag may change and is 1 only for a multi-device credential that is currently backed up (BE 0 with BS 1 is not allowed); relying parties are RECOMMENDED to store the latest values. This server stores both at registration and does not refresh them at sign-in, which `PasskeyOut.backedUp` now says | Verified | w3.org/TR/webauthn-3 §6.1.3 and the Backup Eligibility and Backup State definitions | 2026-09-23 |
| WebAuthn L3 challenges and timeouts: challenges MUST be random, SHOULD be at least 16 bytes, SHOULD be stored by the relying party until the ceremony completes, and SHOULD stay valid for about the upper limit of the recommended ceremony timeout; §15.1 recommends 300000 to 600000 ms with a default of 300000 ms (5 minutes). This server's challenges are 64 bytes and single use, but `CHALLENGE_TTL_SECONDS` defaults to 120 (options `timeout` 120000 ms), below that range: a person who takes longer than two minutes gets `challenge_expired` and starts again. | axios 1.x fetch adapter: `adapter: 'fetch'` is chosen per request or per instance, and it "supports additional response types such as `stream`", so `responseType: 'stream'` hands back the response body as a `ReadableStream` as it arrives (the XHR adapter cannot). Delegated Regulation (EU) 2017/565 Article 65: a firm providing portfolio management or reception and transmission of orders acts in the client's best interests when placing or transmitting orders with other entities for execution, takes all sufficient steps to obtain the best possible result, keeps a policy and monitors it (65(3) to (7)). Delegated Regulation (EU) 2017/589 (RTS 6) Article 15(1): pre-trade controls on order entry for all financial instruments, namely price collars, maximum order values, maximum order volumes and maximum message limits; Article 29: in force on the twentieth day after publication, applying from 3 January 2018. Both delegated regulations published OJ L 87, 31 March 2017, so in force 20 April 2017 | Verified | legislation.gov.uk/eur/2015/751 (articles 3, 4, 18, as adopted; data.xml: OJ JOL_2015_123_R_0001, PDF dated 2015-05-19), /eur/2017/565 (articles 64, 65, 91; OJ JOL_2017_087_R_0001), /eur/2017/589 (articles 15, 29; OJ JOL_2017_087_R_0025, PDF dated 2017-03-31); eur-lex.europa.eu answered this build with an empty challenge page | 2026-09-25 |
| acc-mcp-tools, MCP `tools/call` in revisions 2026-07-28 and 2025-11-25: params carry `name` and optional `arguments`; a result carries `content` (a `text` block among others) and optionally `structuredContent`, and `isError`. Two error paths: an unknown tool or a request that fails the CallToolRequest schema is a JSON-RPC protocol error, -32602 in the examples; an API failure, an input validation error (a date in the wrong format, a value out of range) or a business-logic error is a tool execution error, a result with `isError: true`, so the model can correct itself. A tool that returns structured content SHOULD also return its serialized JSON in a text block. `structuredContent` is any JSON value in 2026-07-28 and a JSON object in 2025-11-25. Servers MUST validate tool inputs, control access and rate-limit tool calls | Verified (fetched) | https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools, https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/server/tools | 2026-09-25 |
| r2-close-and-readiness, evidence file types (CAS-05, `EVIDENCE_ALLOWED_TYPES`, `apps/cases/evidence.py` `RECOGNISED`): every type the setting allows is registered with IANA. `application/pdf` [RFC 8118]; the three Office Open XML types `application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document`, `.spreadsheetml.sheet` and `.presentationml.presentation` [Makoto_Murata], specification ISO/IEC 29500; `image/png` [W3C][PNG_WG][PNG]; `image/jpeg` [RFC 2045][RFC 2046]; `text/plain` [RFC 2046][RFC 3676][RFC 5147]; `text/csv` [RFC 4180][RFC 7111] | Verified | IANA registries `media-types/application.csv`, `image.csv`, `text.csv` | 2026-09-27 |
| r2-close-and-readiness, the bytes `identify()` requires of each evidence type. PDF: RFC 8118 section 8, "All PDF files start with the characters "%PDF-" followed by the PDF version number", extension `.pdf`. Office Open XML: the IANA template for the wordprocessing type gives the magic number "50 4b 03 04 as in ZIP" and the extension `docx` (the code opens the zip's directory and asks for `[Content_Types].xml` and a part under `word/`, `xl/` or `ppt/`). PNG: W3C PNG section 5.2, the signature `89 50 4E 47 0D 0A 1A 0A`. JPEG: the Library of Congress format description (fdd000017), "The first two bytes of every JPEG stream are the Start Of Image (SOI) marker with values 0xFF 0xD8"; the code also asks for the `FF` that opens the next marker. CSV: RFC 4180's template gives no magic number and the extension `CSV`, so CSV and plain text are recognised as UTF-8 text with no NUL that does not open as markup | Verified | rfc-editor.org RFC 8118 and RFC 4180; iana.org media type template; w3.org/TR/png-3; loc.gov fdd000017 | 2026-09-27 |
| r2f-site-trust, security.txt (RFC 9116): `Contact` and `Expires` MUST be present; `Expires` is RFC 3339 and RECOMMENDED less than a year ahead; `Preferred-Languages` is a comma-separated list; `Canonical` names where the file lives; the file sits at `/.well-known/security.txt`, MUST be served over https as `text/plain` with charset utf-8; an OpenPGP signature is RECOMMENDED, not required (not signed here) | Verified (fetched) | https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9116.txt | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-site-trust, robots.txt (RFC 9309): the most specific match (most octets) wins, and an equal allow beats disallow; `$` ends a pattern and `*` matches any run of characters; crawlers MAY read other records such as `Sitemap`; a 4xx robots.txt lets a crawler access anything. So `Allow: /$`, `Allow: /welcome` and `Disallow: /` open exactly the public page | Verified (fetched) | https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9309.txt | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-site-trust, headers: HSTS is ignored over plain HTTP; `preload` needs `includeSubDomains` and a max-age of at least 31536000. `Permissions-Policy: feature=()` disables a feature in the page and every frame; `camera`, `microphone`, `geolocation`, `payment` and `usb` are directives; `publickey-credentials-get` defaults to `self`, so leaving WebAuthn out of the header keeps the passkey ceremonies working (and the public journey signs in with the header on) | Verified (fetched) | developer.mozilla.org, Strict-Transport-Security, Permissions-Policy and its publickey-credentials-get page | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-site-trust, Cloudflare: a www-to-apex redirect is a Single Redirect with a wildcard pattern (request URL `https://www.*`, target `https://${1}`, 301, query string preserved), created at Rules › Overview › Create rule › Redirect Rule, and needs the hostname proxied by Cloudflare; Always Use HTTPS is on SSL/TLS › Edge Certificates and redirects every http request to https | Verified (fetched) | developers.cloudflare.com/rules/url-forwarding/examples/redirect-www-to-root/, /rules/url-forwarding/single-redirects/create-dashboard/, /ssl/edge-certificates/additional-options/always-use-https/ | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-site-trust, bleqq.com from outside: `www` A and AAAA are Cloudflare addresses and answer Railway's fallback 404 (`x-railway-fallback: true`); `http://bleqq.com/` already 301s to https; TXT `v=spf1 include:secureserver.net -all` and a `brevo-code`; MX `secureserver.net`; `_dmarc` `p=none` with reports to `rua@dmarc.brevo.com`; `spf.brevo.com` publishes Brevo's SPF record (same as `spf.sendinblue.com`) | Verified (DNS over HTTPS, curl) | dns.google/resolve; curl -I | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-site-trust, Brevo authentication: the domain is verified by a Brevo code TXT and authenticated with DKIM and DMARC records Brevo shows; SPF is required only with a dedicated IP (so `include:spf.brevo.com` aligns the record with the sender rather than being a Brevo requirement) | Secondary sources; read Brevo's domain page when adding DKIM | Brevo help search results, developers.brevo.com/docs/domain-authentication-and-verification (help.brevo.com answered this build 403) | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-site-trust, where to ask for categorisation: Zscaler `https://sitereview.zscaler.com/` ("Site Review - URL Category Lookup Tool"); Palo Alto Networks `https://urlfiltering.paloaltonetworks.com/` ("Test A Site", with a request-change button under a result); Broadcom `https://sitereview.bluecoat.com/` ("Symantec Sitereview"); Cisco Talos, through `https://support.talosintelligence.com`, whose content-categorisation ticket is `https://talosintelligence.com/reputation_center/web_categorization` ("request a category change"); Fortinet `https://www.fortiguard.com/webfilter` ("Web Filter Lookup", submit with a contact email); Forcepoint `https://support.forcepoint.com/s/site-lookup` (Site Lookup, usable as a guest to request recategorisation; `csi.forcepoint.com` did not resolve from this build) | Verified (fetched), Forcepoint from its support site's search results since its pages render only in a browser | The six vendors' sites | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-host-split, Railway custom domains: the service's Settings › Public Networking › `+ Custom Domain`; Railway shows a `CNAME` (for example `g05ns7.up.railway.app`) and a `TXT` for ownership, both required ("If the `TXT` record is missing, requests to your custom domain will return a `404` error even after the `CNAME` resolves"); a Let's Encrypt certificate is issued automatically, typically within an hour; behind Cloudflare's proxy SSL/TLS must be Full ("Full (Strict) will not work as intended"). The page says nothing about which request headers reach the service, so the owner's check after the switch proves the Host header arrives (a 301 from `bleqq.com/sign-in`) | Verified (fetched) | https://docs.railway.com/networking/domains/working-with-domains | 2026-09-28 |
| r2f-host-split, Next.js 16 `proxy.ts` (the renamed middleware): one exported `proxy` function and an optional `config.matcher`, run on the Node.js runtime before routes; runs after `next.config` headers and redirects; `NextResponse.redirect` and `rewrite` produce the response. Read from the installed package's own docs (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/proxy.md`, 16.3.5). From its source: the URL the proxy sees is built from `localhost` and the listening port, and a redirect to that origin is written as relative (`server/web/adapter.js`, `server/lib/router-utils/resolve-routes.js`), which is why the E2E split server sits behind a forwarder | Verified (installed package docs and source) | next 16.3.5 | 2026-09-28 |

## Library baseline (D-118, ADR 0065, researched 2026-09-30)

One row per instrument of `backend/apps/library/baseline/`, grouped by tranche, naming the
pages each fact was read from. EUR-Lex answered this build with a challenge page, so EU
acts were read through the Publications Office (the metadata notice and the Official
Journal text served by `publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/<CELEX>`), which carries the
same act; each record still cites its EUR-Lex ELI page for a reader. Every EU instrument's
official English title, ELI, entry into force and in-force status was then cross-checked
against its notice by a script, independently of the research: all agree, and the three
apparent differences are explained (Directive (EU) 2015/849's `inForceTo` is the day after
the notice's last day of validity, by the baseline's own rule; Delegated Regulation (EU)
2016/522 carries the title Delegated Regulation (EU) 2026/788 gave it; the DLT Pilot's
notice lists a stray 2021 date beside its entry into force on 2022-06-22).
